## Read this first

- None of these results has been peer reviewed. They are preprints by one independent author, produced with disclosed AI assistance.
- "Lean kernel" means the Lean 4 kernel accepted the proof. That certifies the formal statement, not the choice of statement, so every result also records a statement audit.
- The audit recorded for each result was run by AI review agents in sessions separate from the ones that wrote the proofs, under the author's direction: a clean rebuild at the cited commit, a reading of the formal statement against the claim and its cited source, and a literature search. It is an internal check, not external review.
- A result answers only the question stated. Parent problems named in the papers remain open unless they are listed here.
- 0 of 2,000 catalogued open problems in the Unsolved Problems Mega-Atlas are solved by this work.

## A. Open questions from the literature, answered

*A question or conjecture that another author stated as open in a cited paper or preprint (the citation says which), answered here within the scope given.*

<a id="r1"></a>
### R1. Closed form of the adjacent second-order constant a_△ (Banić–Elezović Conjecture 7.7)

- **Statement.** Let h(ℓ) = E|X₂−X₁|·|X₃−X₂| for (X₁,X₂,X₃) ~ Mult(ℓ; 1/3,1/3,1/3), c_L = 2√3/(3π) + 1/9, a_* = −2√3/(9π). The limits b_r = lim ℓ·(h(ℓ) − c_L·ℓ − a_*) along ℓ ≡ r (mod 3) exist, with b₀ = 32√3/(81π) and b₁ = b₂ = −22√3/(81π). Hence a_△ := 1/32 + (π/9)(b₀+b₁+b₂) = 1/32 − 4√3/243 and γ₂ := (64a_△−3)/(32π) = −1/(32π) − 8√3/(243π).
- **Asked in.** [N. Banić, N. Elezović, Discrete total variation under the multinomial, Part 2: variance under the uniform null (2026), arXiv:2610.17046](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17046), Conjecture 7.7.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`MultinomialTV.ComparatorTarget.target_conjecture_7_7`](https://github.com/0thernes/multinomial-tv-conjecture/blob/0a69443e1b663f0874b547481b7395fdd24732c4/lean/Solution.lean#L129), [`MultinomialTV.ComparatorTarget.target_tendsto_hAdj_mod0`](https://github.com/0thernes/multinomial-tv-conjecture/blob/0a69443e1b663f0874b547481b7395fdd24732c4/lean/Solution.lean#L81), [`MultinomialTV.ComparatorTarget.target_tendsto_hAdj_mod1`](https://github.com/0thernes/multinomial-tv-conjecture/blob/0a69443e1b663f0874b547481b7395fdd24732c4/lean/Solution.lean#L97), [`MultinomialTV.ComparatorTarget.target_tendsto_hAdj_mod2`](https://github.com/0thernes/multinomial-tv-conjecture/blob/0a69443e1b663f0874b547481b7395fdd24732c4/lean/Solution.lean#L113), [`MultinomialTV.conjecture_7_7`](https://github.com/0thernes/multinomial-tv-conjecture/blob/0a69443e1b663f0874b547481b7395fdd24732c4/lean/MultinomialTVConjecture/Main.lean#L320); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement matches the stated claim; prior work: no earlier proof found.
- **Re-audit pending.** Audited at commit `0a69443`; the archive pin has since moved to `5b3d510`.
- **Limit.** The source is an arXiv v1 preprint posted 2026-10-12 and not peer reviewed; this proof is also unreviewed and no independent mathematician has compared the 143-line statement file with the source line by line.
- **Limit.** Lean proves the three limits, not the O(ℓ⁻²) remainder that the source's Lemma 7.3 asserts.
- **Limit.** The step from a_△ to the variance of the total-variation statistic (source Lemma 7.5 and Theorem 7.6) is not formalized.
- **Limit.** The prior-work search (arXiv, Semantic Scholar citations, one web search, 2026-11-26) found only the source series; MathSciNet and zbMATH were not searched.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake exe cache get && lake build && lake env lean VerifyAxioms.lean`
- **Source.** [multinomial-tv-conjecture](https://github.com/0thernes/multinomial-tv-conjecture) `v2.7.0` · archive entry `0TH-2026-006` · [paper](papers/0TH-2026-006_multinomial-tv-conjecture/Donahue_2026_Conjecture_7_7_Multinomial_TV.pdf)

<a id="r44"></a>
### R44. Contextuality degree of k disjoint Peres–Mermin squares and Mermin pentagrams

- **Statement.** For every k ≥ 0: on k vertex-disjoint Peres–Mermin squares (9 points, 6 signed lines each), with contexts the nonempty unions of at most one line per copy and product signs, the minimum number of violated contexts over ±1 assignments is (7ᵏ−5ᵏ)/2 of 7ᵏ−1. For Mermin pentagrams (10 points, 5 lines) it is (6ᵏ−4ᵏ)/2 of 6ᵏ−1. Hence 2d_k/|H_k| → 1.
- **Asked in.** [A. Muller, M. Saniga, Automated search for highly contextual Kochen-Specker proofs (2026), arXiv:2609.19862](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19862v1), Section 6 (Discussion and Outlooks), second paragraph, final clause.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`ContextualityTensor.square_minimum_closed_form`](https://github.com/0thernes/pauli-contextuality-lab/blob/b0f8a180f441674c439840d5125e2c4dcac55a18/lean/ContextualityTensor.lean#L243), [`ContextualityTensor.pentagram_minimum_closed_form`](https://github.com/0thernes/pauli-contextuality-lab/blob/b0f8a180f441674c439840d5125e2c4dcac55a18/lean/ContextualityTensor.lean#L254), [`ContextualityTensor.global_actual_square_minimum`](https://github.com/0thernes/pauli-contextuality-lab/blob/b0f8a180f441674c439840d5125e2c4dcac55a18/lean/ContextualityTensor/ActualModel.lean#L88), [`ContextualityTensor.global_actual_pentagram_minimum`](https://github.com/0thernes/pauli-contextuality-lab/blob/b0f8a180f441674c439840d5125e2c4dcac55a18/lean/ContextualityTensor/ActualModel.lean#L103), [`ContextualityTensor.actual_square_epsilon_tends_to_one`](https://github.com/0thernes/pauli-contextuality-lab/blob/b0f8a180f441674c439840d5125e2c4dcac55a18/lean/ContextualityTensor/ActualModel.lean#L140), [`ContextualityTensor.actual_pentagram_epsilon_tends_to_one`](https://github.com/0thernes/pauli-contextuality-lab/blob/b0f8a180f441674c439840d5125e2c4dcac55a18/lean/ContextualityTensor/ActualModel.lean#L145), [`PauliContextualityLab.ComparatorTarget.target_disjoint_square_degree`](https://github.com/0thernes/pauli-contextuality-lab/blob/b0f8a180f441674c439840d5125e2c4dcac55a18/lean/Solution.lean#L104), [`PauliContextualityLab.ComparatorTarget.target_disjoint_pentagram_degree`](https://github.com/0thernes/pauli-contextuality-lab/blob/b0f8a180f441674c439840d5125e2c4dcac55a18/lean/Solution.lean#L113); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound. Identification with the source's hypergraph support HS(kG) rests on Python enumeration (k ≤ 2) and the source's Sec. 4.2, not on Lean.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement is narrower than the usual informal claim (see Limits); prior work: no earlier proof found.
- **Re-audit pending.** Audited at commit `b0f8a18`; the archive pin has since moved to `9d57651`.
- **Limit.** Only homogeneous copies of the square and of the pentagram are covered; the two doily copies 2L(K6) named in the same source sentence, mixed unions and the other Section 6 outlooks are not.
- **Limit.** No Lean theorem identifies the hard-coded seed lines with HS(L(K3,3)) or HS(Petersen), and the pentagram signs have no Pauli-matrix check in Lean (squares are checked for k = 2 only).
- **Limit.** The source is an unreviewed arXiv v1 preprint and states this as an open question in its discussion, not as a conjecture; the prior-work search (arXiv, Semantic Scholar, Zenodo, web; 2026-11-26) was bounded.

<a id="r45"></a>
### R45. Strict-law failure in the nine vertex cases of Jonah's Conjecture E, every N ≥ 4

- **Statement.** For every N ≥ 4 and each of nine explicit pairs (X, d), with X one of W(2N−1,2), Q⁺, Q⁻ or a perp-section of these, there are ±1 carriers σ, τ whose section operators Σσ(s)P_s and Στ(s)P_s have equal characteristic polynomials (equal eigenvalue multisets), while the completion matrices Λ_σ, Λ_τ on the points anticommuting with d have different characteristic polynomials.
- **Asked in.** [B. W. Jonah, The reach of the section-square law: hyperplane completions of symplectic and quadric Pauli configurations (2026), Zenodo preprint v0.5, doi:10.5281/zenodo.23524332](https://zenodo.org/records/23524332), Section 6, Conjecture E.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`HoggarBound.ConjectureE.jonah_conjecture_e_all_nine_cases`](https://github.com/0thernes/pauli-contextuality-lab/blob/b0f8a180f441674c439840d5125e2c4dcac55a18/lean/HoggarBound/ConjectureE.lean#L2270), [`HoggarBound.AuditUpgrades.jonah_conjecture_e_all_nine_cases_spectra`](https://github.com/0thernes/pauli-contextuality-lab/blob/b0f8a180f441674c439840d5125e2c4dcac55a18/lean/HoggarBound/AuditUpgrades.lean#L1060), [`HoggarBound.ConjectureE.completionMat`](https://github.com/0thernes/pauli-contextuality-lab/blob/b0f8a180f441674c439840d5125e2c4dcac55a18/lean/HoggarBound/ConjectureECore.lean#L48), [`PauliContextualityLab.ComparatorTarget.target_jonah_conjecture_e_all_nine_cases`](https://github.com/0thernes/pauli-contextuality-lab/blob/b0f8a180f441674c439840d5125e2c4dcac55a18/lean/Solution.lean#L76); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound. Case labels for the nine pairs and coverage of whole cases (Jonah's Theorem B4, single orbits) are outside Lean.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement is narrower than the usual informal claim (see Limits); prior work: overlapping earlier or parallel work found (see Limits).
- **Re-audit pending.** Audited at commit `b0f8a18`; the archive pin has since moved to `9d57651`.
- **Limit.** Lean proves failure for one explicit representative per case; that each representative lies in the named case is read off the source's tables, and transfer to every hyperplane of a case uses the source's Theorem B4, neither formalized.
- **Limit.** The source already proves cases C1 and C6 for every n ≥ 3 and reported N = 4 counterexample pairs for six further cases; case C3 for N ≥ 4 was open there.
- **Limit.** The source is an unreviewed, AI-assisted and AI-refereed Zenodo preprint; its conjecture, definitions and Theorem B4 were read in this audit through a PDF text-extraction tool.
- **Limit.** No later resolution was found in a bounded Zenodo and web search (2026-11-26).

<a id="section-b"></a>
## B. New theorems

*A statement first formulated in this work. No earlier statement of it was found in the literature search recorded for the result.*

<a id="r2"></a>
### R2. Digit-sum characterisation of two-register passive register programs with F_p constants

- **Statement.** For p prime, t ≥ 1, f ∈ F_p[x]: a passive two-register program (instructions O += P(R), R += c, O += λx, R += λx; constants in F_p) with at most t input accesses producing O + f(x) and restoring R, as an identity of formal polynomials in F_p[R, x], exists exactly when every d ≥ 2 with [x^d]f ≠ 0 has base-p digit sum s_p(d) ≤ min(p−1, t−1).
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`Catalytic.Solution.t1_digit_sum_dichotomy`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Solution.lean#L17), [`Catalytic.Challenge.T1_DigitSumDichotomy`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Challenge.lean#L21), [`Catalytic.rp_pass_two_iff`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Catalytic/DigitSumDichotomy.lean#L952), [`Catalytic.CleanlyComputes2`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Catalytic/DigitSumDichotomy.lean#L117), [`Catalytic.Solution.t1s_pass2_ring_soundness`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Solution.lean#L37); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound. Formal identity implies correct execution in every commutative ring of characteristic p (proved, T1s); the converse step is a paper argument.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement matches the stated claim; prior work: no earlier proof found.
- **Limit.** Cleanliness means equality of formal polynomials; as functions on F_p itself x^p = x, so for example x^3 over F_2 is computed with one input access although the theorem excludes it.
- **Limit.** Program constants lie in the prime field; with F_4 constants the criterion changes (x^3 becomes computable), and the case of general F_(p^e) is open.
- **Limit.** Vinciguerra (arXiv:2609.18692) proves the characteristic-0 analogue (Theorem 3.5) and does not pose the positive-characteristic question; this is a self-posed extension, not an answer to a published question.
- **Limit.** Two registers only; nothing is shown for three or more registers in positive characteristic.
- **Limit.** That literal clean execution over a polynomial ring implies the formal predicate (needed to read the 'only if' half as a statement about executions) is not formalised.
- **Limit.** Prior-work search was limited to arXiv, Semantic Scholar and one web query on 2026-11-26.
- **Upstream.** The source repository has commits newer than the archived pin. They are not covered by this audit.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake build && lake env lean VerifyAxioms.lean`
- **Source.** [catalytic-register-frontiers](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers) `v1.0.1` · archive entry `0TH-2026-010` · [paper](papers/0TH-2026-010_catalytic-register-frontiers/Donahue_2026_catalytic_register_frontiers.pdf)

<a id="r3"></a>
### R3. Two-access programs for x^(2^k) in characteristic 2 using product updates only

- **Statement.** For k ≥ 1 and every commutative ring with 2 = 0, a program on k+1 registers with instructions R_d += R_a·R_b (d ∉ {a, b}; register 0 never read) and R_d += x uses 2 input accesses and 4k−2 products, adds x^(2^k) to register 0 and restores the others, from every initial state. With k+2 registers and copies R_d += R_s, the indices d, a, b are pairwise distinct.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`Catalytic.Solution.t2_bilinear_frobenius_collapse`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Solution.lean#L49), [`Catalytic.Challenge.T2_BilinearFrobeniusCollapse`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Challenge.lean#L97), [`Catalytic.Solution.t2b_distinct_operand_frobenius`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Solution.lean#L56), [`Catalytic.Challenge.T2b_DistinctOperandFrobenius`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Challenge.lean#L111), [`Catalytic.frobBilinProg`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Catalytic/BilinearFrobenius.lean#L105); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound. Elementary: repeated squaring with (a+b)² = a² + b² in characteristic 2.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement matches the stated claim; prior work: no earlier proof found.
- **Limit.** Characteristic 2 only.
- **Limit.** No lower bound on registers, products or input accesses is proved.
- **Limit.** The product-only instruction set is the repository's restriction of the register-program model; the statement is not tied to a published question.
- **Limit.** Only general searches of the register-program literature were run; the underlying identity is standard.
- **Upstream.** The source repository has commits newer than the archived pin. They are not covered by this audit.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake build && lake env lean VerifyAxioms.lean`
- **Source.** [catalytic-register-frontiers](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers) `v1.0.1` · archive entry `0TH-2026-010` · [paper](papers/0TH-2026-010_catalytic-register-frontiers/Donahue_2026_catalytic_register_frontiers.pdf)

<a id="r4"></a>
### R4. Four-register passive powering of x^N with fewer than (104/3)·N^(log₁₃ 8) input accesses

- **Statement.** Let N ≥ 1 have ℓ+1 base-13 digits. Over every commutative ring in which D_8 = 2656207148788801777680, 12! and (ℓ+1)! are invertible, some passive four-register program maps (o, u, r₁, r₂) to (o + x^N, u, r₁, r₂) with fewer than (104/3)·8^ℓ ≤ (104/3)·N^(log₁₃ 8) input accesses. Counting ℓ+1 base-7 digits instead: 6! and (ℓ+1)! invertible, fewer than (70/3)·5^ℓ accesses. The base-13 bound is also stated coordinatewise over Rⁿ.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`Catalytic.Solution.t3h_four_reg_powering`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Solution.lean#L83), [`Catalytic.Challenge.T3h_FourRegPowering`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Challenge.lean#L181), [`Catalytic.four_reg_powering_x13`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Catalytic/FourRegisterPowering.lean#L1162), [`Catalytic.four_reg_powering_x7`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Catalytic/FourRegisterPowering.lean#L1198), [`Catalytic.Solution.t3i_streaming_frequency_moment`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Solution.lean#L89); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound. Lean states 3·accesses < 104·8^ℓ with 13^ℓ ≤ N; the real-exponent form and the comparison with log₅ 4 are paper-level conversions.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement matches the stated claim; prior work: no earlier proof found.
- **Limit.** Improves the exponent of Corollary 4.3.1 of arXiv:2609.18692 at four registers (log₅ 4 ≈ 0.8614 to log₁₃ 8 ≈ 0.8107); with the stated constants the bound is smaller than 20·N^(log₅ 4) only for N above about 52,000 (base-7 variant: above about 90).
- **Limit.** No lower bound is proved: log₁₃ 8 is the smallest exponent among the constructed base programs (t = 8 minimises log_(2t−3) t over integers t ≥ 4, which is integer arithmetic), not a proven optimum for three-register base programs.
- **Limit.** In prime characteristic p the hypotheses exclude p ∈ {2, 3, 5, 7, 11, 137, 1602207194491} and require p > ℓ+1, which limits N.
- **Limit.** With five registers the cited paper already has the smaller exponent log₇ 4 ≈ 0.712.
- **Limit.** The coordinatewise corollary contains no stream model or space bound; the streaming and matrix-powering applications are paper arguments outside Lean.
- **Upstream.** The source repository has commits newer than the archived pin. They are not covered by this audit.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake build && lake env lean VerifyAxioms.lean`

<a id="r5"></a>
### R5. Lower bounds for the Shannon capacity of the odd cycles C₂₅ and C₂₉

- **Statement.** C₂₅^⊠144 contains an independent set of size M with 12344196091426841^144 ≤ M·10^(15·144), and C₂₉^⊠4 contains an independent set of size 42050, where 14319949949077991^4 ≤ 42050·10^60. Consequently Θ(C₂₅) ≥ 12.344196091426841 and Θ(C₂₉) ≥ 14.319949949077991. Both exceed the largest earlier values found, 23125^(1/4) ≈ 12.3316 and 42017^(1/4) ≈ 14.3171 (Codenotti–Gerace–Resta 2003, Table 1).
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`ZeroErrorFrontier.WorldRecords.t2a_c25_world_record`](https://github.com/0thernes/zero-error-torus-frontier/blob/af343aaa1701637e23733ac0abd7a8f9b2e22b60/lean/ZeroErrorFrontier/WorldRecords.lean#L446), [`ZeroErrorFrontier.WorldRecords.t2c_c29_world_record`](https://github.com/0thernes/zero-error-torus-frontier/blob/af343aaa1701637e23733ac0abd7a8f9b2e22b60/lean/ZeroErrorFrontier/WorldRecords.lean#L514); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound. Θ is not defined in Lean; the capacity bounds follow from the integer statements by Θ(G) = sup α(G^⊠d)^(1/d).
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement matches the stated claim; prior work: no earlier proof found.
- **Limit.** Lower bounds only; the exact value of Θ(C_n) is open for every odd n ≥ 7.
- **Limit.** The margins over the 2003 values are 0.01257 (C₂₅) and 0.00281 (C₂₉, 33 more words in the fourth power), smaller than the gains the repository states against third-power values.
- **Limit.** The C₂₅ base set has the 2003 published size 23125; the gain comes from a port system and the Buys–Polak–Zuiddam substitution tables, whose framework this follows.
- **Limit.** The prior-work search was not exhaustive: Baumert et al. 1971 and Hales 1973 were not opened, and the 2003 table entries are printed cardinalities.
- **Upstream.** The source repository has commits newer than the archived pin. They are not covered by this audit.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake build && lake env lean VerifyAxioms.lean`
- **Source.** [zero-error-torus-frontier](https://github.com/0thernes/zero-error-torus-frontier) `v1.0.0` · archive entry `0TH-2026-016` · [paper](papers/0TH-2026-016_zero-error-torus-frontier/Donahue_2026_zero_error_torus_frontier.pdf)

<a id="r6"></a>
### R6. Independent sets in the fourth strong power of C_n for n = 4m+1

- **Statement.** For every m ≥ 5 with m ≡ 0 or 1 (mod 4) and n = 4m+1: α(C_n^⊠4) ≥ (m² + ⌊m/4⌋)·n². The set is lifted from m² + ⌊m/4⌋ = ⌊n⌊n/4⌋/4⌋ points of ℤ_n², no two within circular distance 3 in both coordinates; no such point set is larger (n ≥ 4), and each one of size M lifts to M·n² points (n ≥ 9).
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`ZeroErrorFrontier.CosetQuotient.t1d_infinite_helical_hbpz_family`](https://github.com/0thernes/zero-error-torus-frontier/blob/af343aaa1701637e23733ac0abd7a8f9b2e22b60/lean/ZeroErrorFrontier/CosetQuotient.lean#L734), [`ZeroErrorFrontier.CosetQuotient.t1a_coset_quotient_compression`](https://github.com/0thernes/zero-error-torus-frontier/blob/af343aaa1701637e23733ac0abd7a8f9b2e22b60/lean/ZeroErrorFrontier/CosetQuotient.lean#L249), [`ZeroErrorFrontier.CosetQuotient.t1b_exact_quotient_ceilings`](https://github.com/0thernes/zero-error-torus-frontier/blob/af343aaa1701637e23733ac0abd7a8f9b2e22b60/lean/ZeroErrorFrontier/CosetQuotient.lean#L533); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound. Three contract theorems: lifting (T1a), ceiling (T1b), family (T1d).
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement matches the stated claim; prior work: overlapping earlier or parallel work found (see Limits).
- **Limit.** For m ≡ 0 (mod 4), n = 16j+1, the size equals (n⁴−n³)/16, the exact value of α(C_n^⊠4) credited to Baumert et al. (1971) in the 2003 paper of Codenotti, Gerace and Resta.
- **Limit.** The case n = 21 (11466 words, 26 cosets of ⟨(1,0,0,2),(0,1,2,0)⟩) is in the 2026 Lean repository of Buys, Polak and Zuiddam.
- **Limit.** For m ≡ 1 (mod 4), m ≥ 9, the sizes exceed the 2003 table entries at n = 37 and n = 53 (113627 vs 113586, 483148 vs 483091); no search for later work on these n was done.
- **Limit.** The ceiling bounds unions of cosets of one subgroup, not α(C_n^⊠4); Lean proves the lifting direction only.
- **Upstream.** The source repository has commits newer than the archived pin. They are not covered by this audit.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake build && lake env lean VerifyAxioms.lean`

<a id="r46"></a>
### R46. Infinite family of non-closed multi-qubit Pauli sets attaining the spectral floor

- **Statement.** For every m ≥ 1, with q = 2^m, there is a set S of (q³−1)q² non-identity 3m-qubit Pauli labels, containing two distinct commuting elements whose product is not in S, with 6·#lines = (q³−1)q⁴(q²+q−3), whose minimum number of violated lines over ±1 assignments is (q³−1)q³(q−1)(q−2)/12. These counts give equality in the spectral floor; the minimum is positive for m ≥ 2.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`HoggarBound.NormPhaseFamily.infinite_nonclosed_saturated_family`](https://github.com/0thernes/pauli-contextuality-lab/blob/b0f8a180f441674c439840d5125e2c4dcac55a18/lean/HoggarBound/NormPhaseFamily.lean#L1291), [`HoggarBound.NormPhaseFamily.infinite_nonclosed_family_contextual`](https://github.com/0thernes/pauli-contextuality-lab/blob/b0f8a180f441674c439840d5125e2c4dcac55a18/lean/HoggarBound/NormPhaseFamily.lean#L1407), [`HoggarBound.NormPhaseFamily.detPhase_isFlatState`](https://github.com/0thernes/pauli-contextuality-lab/blob/b0f8a180f441674c439840d5125e2c4dcac55a18/lean/HoggarBound/NormPhaseFamily.lean#L974), [`PauliContextualityLab.ComparatorTarget.target_infinite_nonclosed_saturated_family`](https://github.com/0thernes/pauli-contextuality-lab/blob/b0f8a180f441674c439840d5125e2c4dcac55a18/lean/Solution.lean#L52); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound. Floor equality follows from the stated counts by arithmetic (checked in this audit); flatness of the |Ψ₃ₘ⟩ state is a separate Lean theorem.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement matches the stated claim; prior work: no earlier proof found.
- **Re-audit pending.** Audited at commit `b0f8a18`; the archive pin has since moved to `9d57651`.

- **Limit.** The question it answers was posed in the repository's own manuscript, not by another author.

<a id="r52"></a>
### R52. Even-Laplacian parity for quadratic-plus-periodic integer functions on the triangular lattice

- **Statement.** Let M ≥ 1, ψ doubly periodic with periods ≥ 1, and u: ℤ² → ℤ with M·u = Ax² + Bxy + Cy² + Dx + Ey + ψ. If M·K = 4A − 2B + 4C and the six-neighbour triangular-lattice Laplacian of u is ≤ K everywhere, then K is even; so K = 5 is impossible in this class. Explicit functions attain constant Laplacian 4 and 2.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declaration [`ApollonianSandpileMarkov.Palomar.t2d_triangular_a2_even_laplacian_rigidity_and_obstruction`](https://github.com/0thernes/apollonian-sandpile-markov/blob/94ece6a8dce9c9a30c6a2ada2eb335cb0501f770/lean/PalomarSolution.lean#L44); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound. Elementary: the hypotheses force constant Laplacian, and integer-valued quadratics have even A₂ Laplacian.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement is narrower than the usual informal claim (see Limits); prior work: no earlier proof found.
- **Re-audit pending.** Audited at commit `94ece6a`; the archive pin has since moved to `bc6464b`.
- **Limit.** Requires the Laplacian bound K to equal the mean curvature (4A − 2B + 4C)/M; envelopes with smaller mean are not covered.
- **Limit.** Says nothing about sandpile configurations of height 5 or the A₂ sandpile identity (v2.0.0 removed the unsupported causal remark from paper and figures).
- **Limit.** No prior statement found in two literature searches, though the argument is elementary (v2.0.0 also adds a constructive joint NonVacuity witness for the hypothesis bundle).
- **Limit.** The description of integer superharmonic matrices on A₂ (an open problem posed by Levine-Pegden-Smart for periodic graphs) is not addressed.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake build && lake env lean VerifyAxioms.lean`
- **Source.** [apollonian-sandpile-markov](https://github.com/0thernes/apollonian-sandpile-markov) `v3.0.0` · archive entry `0TH-2026-018` · [paper](papers/0TH-2026-018_apollonian-sandpile-markov/Donahue_2026_apollonian_sandpile_markov.pdf)

<a id="r53"></a>
### R53. Signature change of the Cayley–Dickson sign table and 2-blade nullities

- **Statement.** For all n and every signature γ ∈ {±1}ⁿ: σ_n^γ(i,j) = σ_n(i,j)(−1)^popcount(i∧j∧flip(γ)); block signs flip by (−1)^popcount((i⊕j)∧flip(γ)); under bilinear cdMulCoordGamma, (e_i+e_j)² = 0 iff σ_n^γ(i,i)+σ_n^γ(j,j)=0 and ν_n^γ(i,j) equals the cardinality of an explicit ℤ-basis of ker L_{e_i+e_j} (T1f); hence H(v)+H(2^(n−1)−v) and H(2^(n−2)) are signature-invariant.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`CDTerrace.Palomar.prove_t1a`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/PalomarSolution.lean#L20), [`CDTerrace.Palomar.prove_t1b`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/PalomarSolution.lean#L21), - **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement matches the stated claim; prior work: no earlier proof found.
- **Limit.** Applies to canonical 2-blades e_i + e_j (1 ≤ i < j < 2^n); higher-blade elements (3+ basis terms) are not covered by T1a–T1f.
- **Limit.** The cochain factorization T1a is explicitly credited in context to the twisted group-algebra framework of Albuquerque–Majid (1999) and Morier-Genoud–Ovsienko (2011).
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake build && lake env lean VerifyAxioms.lean`

<a id="r54"></a>
### R54. Doubling recurrence and closed forms for Euclidean 2-blade nullities

- **Statement.** In Euclidean CD_n: ν_{n+1} obeys a four-branch doubling recurrence; 4 | ν (n ≥ 3); ν ≤ 2^(n−1)−4 (attained, matching the BDI formula 2^n−4n+4 uniquely at n = 4 and strictly below it for all n ≥ 5); for n ≥ 3, Σν = 8(2^(n−1)−1)(2^(n−2)−1)(2^(n−3)−1), H_n(0) = (6n−9)2^(n−2)+3, H_{n+1} interleaves 2H_n palindromically, and H_{n+1}(2^n−4) = 2H_n(0).
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`CDTerrace.Palomar.prove_t2a`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/PalomarSolution.lean#L26), [`CDTerrace.Palomar.prove_t2b`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/PalomarSolution.lean#L27), [`CDTerrace.Palomar.prove_t2c`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/PalomarSolution.lean#L28), [`CDTerrace.Palomar.prove_t2d`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/PalomarSolution.lean#L29), [`CDTerrace.Palomar.prove_t2e`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/PalomarSolution.lean#L30), [`CDTerrace.nullity_doubling_cross`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/CDTerrace/TerraceLaw.lean#L557), [`CDTerrace.nullity_mod4_and_bound`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/CDTerrace/TerraceLaw.lean#L690), [`CDTerrace.twoblade_strictly_below_bdi`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/CDTerrace/TerraceLaw.lean#L788), [`CDTerrace.twoblade_bdi_eq_iff_four`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/CDTerrace/TerraceLaw.lean#L844), [`CDTerrace.totalNullityMass_all_n`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/CDTerrace/TerraceLaw.lean#L1274), [`CDTerrace.nonZDCount_all_n`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/CDTerrace/TerraceLaw.lean#L1373), [`CDTerrace.terrace_palindrome_all_n`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/CDTerrace/TerraceLaw.lean#L1503), [`CDTerrace.terrace_top_bin_all_n`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/CDTerrace/TerraceLaw.lean#L1518); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement matches the stated claim; prior work: overlapping earlier or parallel work found (see Limits).
- **Limit.** Euclidean signature only for the doubling terrace law; 4 | ν is the 2-blade case of Moreno (1998) / Biss–Dugger–Isaksen (2008).
- **Limit.** The comparison with the BDI ceiling compares the proved 2-blade ceiling 2^(n-1)-4 against the closed BDI formula 2^n-4n+4 (the general-element BDI upper bound itself is cited, not formalized).
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake build && lake env lean VerifyAxioms.lean`

<a id="r55"></a>
### R55. Midpoint bin count 42·2^(n−4) for all Cayley–Dickson signatures

- **Statement.** For every n ≥ 4 and every signature γ ∈ {±1}ⁿ, the number of pairs 1 ≤ i < j < 2ⁿ whose 2-blade e_i+e_j has nullity 2^(n−2) in CD_n(γ) equals 42·2^(n−4).
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`CDTerrace.Palomar.prove_t2f`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/PalomarSolution.lean#L31), [`CDTerrace.terrace_midpoint_std_all_n`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/CDTerrace/TerraceLaw.lean#L1572), [`CDTerrace.universal_split_midpoint_42`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/CDTerrace/TerraceLaw.lean#L1596); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement matches the stated claim; prior work: overlapping earlier or parallel work found (see Limits).
- **Limit.** With T1f (exact ℤ-basis isomorphism for ker L_{e_i+e_j}), the Lean statement now directly certifies the exact annihilator dimension of canonical 2-blades across all signatures.
- **Limit.** The base case n = 4 (42) corresponds to de Marrais (arXiv:math/0011260); the all-n, all-2^n-signature generalization was not found in a bounded search.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake build && lake env lean VerifyAxioms.lean`
- **Source.** [cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier) `v2.0.0` · archive entry `0TH-2026-019` · [paper](papers/0TH-2026-019_cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/Donahue_2026_cayley_dickson_terrace_frontier.pdf)

<a id="r56"></a>
### R56. Coordinate right-multiplier families in Euclidean Cayley–Dickson algebras

- **Statement.** In the Euclidean CD_n: for n ≥ 3 a set of basis indices with pairwise compatible right multiplications (a norm-multiplicative [2ⁿ, s, 2ⁿ] coordinate pair) has at most n+5 elements, attained; for n ≥ 4 only e_0 and e_{2^(n−1)} are compatible with all indices; a [2^(n−1), 2(n+4), 2ⁿ] coordinate pair exists for n ≥ 4; n+5 = ρ(2ⁿ) for n = 3,4,5 and n+5 < ρ(2ⁿ) for n ≥ 6.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`CDTerrace.Palomar.prove_t3a`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/PalomarSolution.lean#L32), [`CDTerrace.Palomar.prove_t3b`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/PalomarSolution.lean#L33), [`CDTerrace.Palomar.prove_t3c`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/PalomarSolution.lean#L34), [`CDTerrace.Palomar.prove_t3d`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/PalomarSolution.lean#L35), [`CDTerrace.universal_right_multipliers_all_n`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/CDTerrace/HurwitzRupture.lean#L358), [`CDTerrace.compat_clique_le_all_n`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/CDTerrace/HurwitzRupture.lean#L550), [`CDTerrace.cdMaxClique_valid`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/CDTerrace/HurwitzRupture.lean#L661), [`CDTerrace.hurwitz_radon_rupture_all_ge6`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/CDTerrace/HurwitzRupture.lean#L722), [`CDTerrace.asymmetric_doubling_tower_all_n`](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/blob/cbd146a3f174a39fe9cfb656d7fd6f93c5bd64be/lean/CDTerrace/HurwitzRupture.lean#L750); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement matches the stated claim; prior work: no earlier proof found.
- **Limit.** Only coordinate (basis-index) subsets with the full basis as left factor in the Euclidean signature; nothing is shown about non-coordinate subspaces or general Hurwitz–Radon bilinear maps.
- **Limit.** The comparison with ρ(2ⁿ) (T3c) is arithmetic on the closed formula 8⌊n/4⌋+2^(n mod 4); its classical identification with the Hurwitz–Radon number is cited (Hurwitz 1922, Radon 1922) and not proved in Lean.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake build && lake env lean VerifyAxioms.lean`
- **Source.** [cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier](https://github.com/0thernes/cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier) `v2.0.0` · archive entry `0TH-2026-019` · [paper](papers/0TH-2026-019_cayley-dickson-terrace-frontier/Donahue_2026_cayley_dickson_terrace_frontier.pdf)

<a id="section-c"></a>
## C. Bounded and partial cases

*An exact answer inside a stated finite range or restricted setting. The unrestricted problem stays open.*

<a id="r7"></a>
### R7. Minimum Kochen-Specker set in the 49-ray integer pool B(3,2)

- **Statement.** Let B(3,2) be the 49 primitive rays of ℤ³ with entries in {−2,…,2}. Some 31 of them admit no Kochen–Specker colouring (no two orthogonal rays coloured 1, every orthogonal triple inside the set containing a ray coloured 1), and every subset of at most 30 of them admits one.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`KS.Box3H2.minimum_ks_size_vectors`](https://github.com/0thernes/kochen-specker-extremal-lab/blob/4c4dda9113f6d7af037d3ab0d7c4d9ca0191fa84/lean/KS/Box3H2.lean#L238), [`KS.ComparatorTarget.target_box3h2_minimum_31`](https://github.com/0thernes/kochen-specker-extremal-lab/blob/4c4dda9113f6d7af037d3ab0d7c4d9ca0191fa84/lean/Solution.lean#L9); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound. The pool list is proved equal to all such rays, each once (rays_eq, boxRays_exact).
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement matches the stated claim; prior work: overlapping earlier or parallel work found (see Limits).
- **Re-audit pending.** Audited at commit `4c4dda9`; the archive pin has since moved to `a433e72`.
- **Limit.** Only rays inside B(3,2) are considered; the unrestricted minimum in dimension three (QM-011) is not affected.
- **Limit.** The value 31 was reported earlier by Kernaghan (arXiv:2603.16988, v1–v9); v10 (Appendix A, item C2) calls the earlier certification code unsound but keeps 31 for this pool after recomputing it.
- **Limit.** An independent Lean proof for the same pool appeared in arXiv:2603.16988v10 on 23 Nov 2026, after this repository's v1.0.0 of 10 Nov 2026; v10 states that its Lean pool list is not proved to contain all rays.
- **Limit.** A ProblemHive forum note (post p/xfkay, October 2026, reference [33] of the repository paper) reported the minimum 31 for integer coordinates up to 2, 3 and 4, without a certificate.
- **Limit.** That there are exactly six minimum sets is not part of the Lean theorem.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake build KS Solution && lake env lean KS/Audit.lean`
- **Source.** [kochen-specker-extremal-lab](https://github.com/0thernes/kochen-specker-extremal-lab) `v2.2.0` · archive entry `0TH-2026-004` · [paper](papers/0TH-2026-004_kochen-specker-extremal-lab/ks-minimum.pdf)

<a id="r8"></a>
### R8. Minimum Kochen-Specker set in the integer pools B(3,3) and B(3,4)

- **Statement.** Among the 145 (H = 3) and the 289 (H = 4) rays of ℤ³ with a representative of max-norm at most H, every set of at most 30 rays admits a Kochen–Specker colouring; a 31-ray set without one exists, since it lies in B(3,2).
- **Evidence.** Checked certificate; axioms: n/a (no Lean proof). B(3,3): drat-trim/LRAT in CI and an independent auditor SAT run. B(3,4): only the project's DRUP checker, not re-run.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; not yet audited; prior work: overlapping earlier or parallel work found (see Limits).
- **Re-audit pending.** Audited at commit `4c4dda9`; the archive pin has since moved to `a433e72`.
- **Limit.** There is no Lean proof (a B(3,3) Lean module was stopped after 49 minutes without a verdict and is not in the repository). B(3,3) rests on the Python CNF encoder with SAT proofs checked by the project's DRUP checker and by drat-trim/LRAT, and on a search-tree certificate replayed by the project's JavaScript verifier; B(3,4) rests on the encoder and the project's DRUP checker.
- **Limit.** The SAT encodings are sound only by paper lemmas of paper/PROOFS.md, not checked by machine: Lemma 1 (colouring cuts), Lemma 3 (conditions C1/C2 for critical sets) and Lemma 6 (symmetry normal form).
- **Limit.** For B(3,4) no proof file is tracked in git and no third-party checker was run.
- **Limit.** Kernaghan (arXiv:2603.16988) already listed the minimum 31 for the 145-ray pool {0, ±1, ±2, ±3} (50 triads) in v1 of 17 Mar 2026 (Table 2), without a certificate; v10 (23 Nov 2026) certifies it.
- **Limit.** A ProblemHive forum note (post p/xfkay, October 2026, reference [33] of the repository paper) reported the minimum 31 for integer coordinates up to 3 and 4, without a certificate.
- **Limit.** The unrestricted minimum in dimension three is not affected.
- **Check it.** `bash scripts/verify_lrat.sh`
- **Source.** [kochen-specker-extremal-lab](https://github.com/0thernes/kochen-specker-extremal-lab) `v2.2.0` · archive entry `0TH-2026-004` · [paper](papers/0TH-2026-004_kochen-specker-extremal-lab/ks-minimum.pdf)

<a id="r9"></a>
### R9. Minimum Kochen-Specker set in roots-of-unity pools U(3,N), N = 6, 12, 18, 24

- **Statement.** For N ∈ {6, 12, 18, 24}, among the rays of ℂ³ whose entries are 0 or N-th roots of unity, every set of at most 32 rays admits a Kochen–Specker colouring, and some 33-ray set admits none.
- **Related source.** [M. Kernaghan, The Algebraic Landscape of Kochen-Specker Sets in Dimension Three (2026), arXiv:2603.16988](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16988), v10, text after Computed fact 16: 'We have not certified the minimum for n > 6'.
- **Evidence.** Checked certificate; axioms: n/a (no Lean proof). Project DRUP checker only. The auditor's independent SAT run confirms N = 6 and N = 12; N = 18 and 24 were not re-run.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; not yet audited; prior work: overlapping earlier or parallel work found (see Limits).
- **Re-audit pending.** Audited at commit `4c4dda9`; the archive pin has since moved to `a433e72`.
- **Limit.** There is no Lean proof; complex orthogonality is decided in Python.
- **Limit.** For N = 6 the value and the count of 3 minimum sets are also in Kernaghan v10, which proves the minimum in Lean.
- **Limit.** Whether the minimum is 33 for every multiple of 6 is open.
- **Limit.** The source states only that n > 6 is uncertified; it does not pose this as a named open problem.
- **Check it.** `NOT AVAILABLE as one command at the pin: the proofs must be regenerated with the repository scripts (README.md line 249: rows A2 to A7 and A9 take seconds to hours to regenerate)`
- **Source.** [kochen-specker-extremal-lab](https://github.com/0thernes/kochen-specker-extremal-lab) `v2.2.0` · archive entry `0TH-2026-004` · [paper](papers/0TH-2026-004_kochen-specker-extremal-lab/ks-minimum.pdf)

<a id="r10"></a>
### R10. Minimum Kochen-Specker set in the 121-ray pool {0, ±1}⁵

- **Statement.** Among the 121 rays of ℤ⁵ with entries in {0, 1, −1}, every set of at most 28 rays admits a Kochen–Specker colouring, and some 29-ray set admits none.
- **Evidence.** Checked certificate; axioms: n/a (no Lean proof). Rests on a paper lemma (Lemma 7, three cases by number of axes) and 83 SAT proofs checked by the project's own checker.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; not yet audited; prior work: overlapping earlier or parallel work found (see Limits).
- **Re-audit pending.** Audited at commit `4c4dda9`; the archive pin has since moved to `a433e72`.
- **Limit.** There is no Lean proof, and no third-party proof checker was run.
- **Limit.** The case split (Lemma 7), the cube decomposition and the lemmas behind the SAT encoding (Lemma 1 colouring cuts, Lemma 3 conditions C1/C2, Lemma 6 symmetry normal form) are paper arguments, not checked by machine.
- **Limit.** Proof files are not tracked in git; re-checking means regenerating them (hours, per the README).
- **Limit.** The value 29 was reported earlier, without a proof that nothing smaller exists, as the smallest critical set generated from the components 0, ±1 in dimension five (Pavičić and Megill, Phys. Rev. A 106, L060203 (2022), arXiv:2202.08197); the new part is the lower bound inside this pool.
- **Limit.** Not re-run by this audit.
- **Limit.** The general minimum in dimension five is not affected.
- **Check it.** `NOT AVAILABLE as one command at the pin: regeneration scripts only (README.md line 249)`
- **Source.** [kochen-specker-extremal-lab](https://github.com/0thernes/kochen-specker-extremal-lab) `v2.2.0` · archive entry `0TH-2026-004` · [paper](papers/0TH-2026-004_kochen-specker-extremal-lab/ks-minimum.pdf)

<a id="r11"></a>
### R11. Input-access counts for x^3, x^5 and x^7 in two-register passive programs over F_4

- **Statement.** For passive two-register programs with constants in the field F_4 and equality of formal polynomials in F_4[R, x]: x^5 is produced with 3 input accesses and not with 2; x^3 is produced with 4 input accesses and not with 3; x^7 is produced with no number of input accesses.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`Catalytic.Solution.t1e_extension_field_separation`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Solution.lean#L32), [`Catalytic.Challenge.T1e_ExtensionFieldSeparation`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Challenge.lean#L54), [`Catalytic.InRPPass2GF4`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Catalytic/ExtensionFieldSeparation.lean#L177), [`Catalytic.gf4_x7_impossible_all_t`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Catalytic/ExtensionFieldSeparation.lean#L371); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound. F_4 is an explicit four-element type with table-defined operations and a proved commutative-ring instance.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement matches the stated claim; prior work: no earlier proof found.
- **Limit.** Three monomials over one field; no general criterion for F_(p^e) is proved.
- **Limit.** Formal-polynomial cleanliness as in T1; as functions on F_4, x^7 = x.
- **Limit.** Not posed in the cited literature.
- **Upstream.** The source repository has commits newer than the archived pin. They are not covered by this audit.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake build && lake env lean VerifyAxioms.lean`
- **Source.** [catalytic-register-frontiers](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers) `v1.0.1` · archive entry `0TH-2026-010` · [paper](papers/0TH-2026-010_catalytic-register-frontiers/Donahue_2026_catalytic_register_frontiers.pdf)

<a id="r12"></a>
### R12. Three-register passive programs for x^(2t−3) with t = 5, …, 9 input accesses

- **Statement.** For t = 5,…,9 and every commutative ring in which the integer D_t is invertible, a passive program on registers (O, u, v) with exactly t input accesses maps (o, u, v) to (o + x^(2t−3), u, v) for all x, o, u, v. D_5 = 576, D_6 = 382500000; D_7, D_8, D_9 have 23, 22 and 51 digits. Hence D^pass_3(t) ≥ 2t−3 for these t over such fields.
- **Related source.** [A. Vinciguerra, An Operator Approach to Register Programs for Catalytic Computing (2026), arXiv:2609.18692v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18692), Sec. 6, paragraph 4: 'Would additional input accesses drastically increase D^pass_{r,K}?'.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`Catalytic.Solution.t3a_three_reg_5call_x7`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Solution.lean#L62), [`Catalytic.Solution.t3b_three_reg_6call_x9`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Solution.lean#L65), [`Catalytic.Solution.t3c_three_reg_7call_x11`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Solution.lean#L68), [`Catalytic.Solution.t3d_three_reg_8call_x13`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Solution.lean#L71), [`Catalytic.Solution.t3g_three_reg_9call_x15`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Solution.lean#L80), [`Catalytic.prog8_x13`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Catalytic/ThreeRegisterBreakthrough.lean#L496); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound. Also re-derived in this audit by exact integer polynomial arithmetic outside Lean: each program adds D_t·x^(2t−3) and restores u, v.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement is narrower than the usual informal claim (see Limits); prior work: no earlier proof found.
- **Limit.** Lower bounds for three registers and five values of t only; the cited question is not answered: no upper bound on D^pass_3(t) is proved, and D^pass_3(4) is itself only known to be at least 5.
- **Limit.** The companion question of the same section, whether D^pass_r(4) exceeds 2r−1, is not addressed.
- **Limit.** No uniform family: nothing is constructed for t ≥ 10.
- **Limit.** Excluded characteristics are the prime factors of D_t (for t = 8: 2, 3, 5, 7, 137, 1602207194491).
- **Limit.** The cited source is an arXiv preprint (v1), not a refereed publication.
- **Limit.** Prior-work search limited to arXiv, Semantic Scholar and one web query on 2026-11-26.
- **Upstream.** The source repository has commits newer than the archived pin. They are not covered by this audit.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake build && lake env lean VerifyAxioms.lean`
- **Source.** [catalytic-register-frontiers](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers) `v1.0.1` · archive entry `0TH-2026-010` · [paper](papers/0TH-2026-010_catalytic-register-frontiers/Donahue_2026_catalytic_register_frontiers.pdf)

<a id="r47"></a>
### R47. Exact binary completion optima 368 and 344 for one four-qubit C3 sign pair

- **Statement.** On the four-qubit hyperbolic quadric Q⁺(7,2) cut by the hyperplane of one fixed point (71 section points, 64 outside points), for two explicit ±1 sign patterns on the section, each with four −1 entries, the minimum over ±1 values on the 64 outside points of the number of violated outside lines is 368 and 344 respectively, each attained.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`HoggarBound.C3ExactOptima.corollary16_exact_binary_optima`](https://github.com/0thernes/pauli-contextuality-lab/blob/b0f8a180f441674c439840d5125e2c4dcac55a18/lean/HoggarBound/C3ExactOptima.lean#L931), [`HoggarBound.ConjectureE.c3ViolatedOutsideContexts`](https://github.com/0thernes/pauli-contextuality-lab/blob/b0f8a180f441674c439840d5125e2c4dcac55a18/lean/HoggarBound/ConjectureEExtras.lean#L581), [`PauliContextualityLab.ComparatorTarget.target_c3_exact_binary_optima`](https://github.com/0thernes/pauli-contextuality-lab/blob/b0f8a180f441674c439840d5125e2c4dcac55a18/lean/Solution.lean#L65); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound. Lower bounds use integer SDP-dual certificates verified inside the Lean kernel by decide +kernel.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement matches the stated claim; prior work: not yet searched.
- **Re-audit pending.** Audited at commit `b0f8a18`; the archive pin has since moved to `9d57651`.
- **Limit.** A single finite instance (N = 4, two fixed sign patterns); no statement for other N or other pairs.
- **Limit.** Jonah's preprint asks only for a cospectral C3 pair with different completion spectra; these optima are a self-posed additional statement.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake exe cache get && lake build HoggarBound ContextualityTensor Evidence Solution VerifyAll && lake env lean VerifyAll.lean`
- **Source.** [pauli-contextuality-lab](https://github.com/0thernes/pauli-contextuality-lab) `v3.2.0` · archive entry `0TH-2026-009` · [paper](papers/0TH-2026-009_pauli-contextuality-lab/unified-contextuality.pdf)

<a id="r57"></a>
### R57. Sign-point determinants and the sign lifts of Gardam's support

- **Statement.** For all integers P, Q, R, S, the determinant of the defined 4×4 matrix blochAtSign(P,Q,R,S) at each point (±1,±1,±1) equals a stated product of linear forms, a square, or a product of sums of two squares. For every ±1 choice on Gardam's 21 support coefficients these eight determinants are not all ±1, also when the p-part is any function even at sign points.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`PromislowFracton.Palomar.t1d_eight_sign_point_factorization`](https://github.com/0thernes/promislow-fracton-amoeba/blob/c829ab811568a13e2283db2175f684e35b17c816/lean/PalomarSolution.lean#L25), [`PromislowFracton.Palomar.t1f_exhaustive_no_sign_lift_gardam_support`](https://github.com/0thernes/promislow-fracton-amoeba/blob/c829ab811568a13e2283db2175f684e35b17c816/lean/PalomarSolution.lean#L29), [`PromislowFracton.eight_sign_point_factorization`](https://github.com/0thernes/promislow-fracton-amoeba/blob/c829ab811568a13e2283db2175f684e35b17c816/lean/PromislowFracton/BlochMatrix.lean#L558), [`PromislowFracton.strengthened_no_sign_lift_of_gardam_support`](https://github.com/0thernes/promislow-fracton-amoeba/blob/c829ab811568a13e2283db2175f684e35b17c816/lean/PromislowFracton/GardamUnit.lean#L460); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound. 'Unit in ℤ[P] ⇒ all sign-point determinants are ±1' is a paper argument; Lean has no determinant multiplicativity.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement is narrower than the usual informal claim (see Limits); prior work: overlapping earlier or parallel work found (see Limits).
- **Limit.** Lean does not state that the lifts are non-units in ℤ[P]; it states that a necessary determinant test fails.
- **Limit.** blochAtSign is a separate definition with no lemma linking it to the Bloch evaluation map.
- **Limit.** Tabei, arXiv:2608.00103 (2026-07-30, preprint), proves that no unit of ℤ[P] supported in B(4) has nontrivial reduction mod 2, which implies the non-unit conclusion for all these lifts.
- **Limit.** Whether ℤ[P] has any non-trivial unit remains open (Gardam, arXiv:2312.05240, found none).
- **Upstream.** The source repository has commits newer than the archived pin. They are not covered by this audit.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake build && lake env lean VerifyAxioms.lean`
- **Source.** [promislow-fracton-amoeba](https://github.com/0thernes/promislow-fracton-amoeba) `v1.0.0` · archive entry `0TH-2026-015` · [paper](papers/0TH-2026-015_promislow-fracton-amoeba/Donahue_2026_promislow_fracton_amoeba.pdf)

### R58. Finite computations for signed Haah conics and odd-order CC1 points over F_p

- **Statement.** On the 64 sign tuples, the defined function signedHaahConicDet takes the values −12, −8, −4, 4, 8, 12 with counts 12, 16, 4, 4, 16, 12, none divisible by a prime p ≥ 5. No (x,y,z) ∈ (F_p^×)³ of odd order satisfies 1+x+y+z = 0 and 1+xy+yz+zx = 0 for p = 3, 5, 7, 11, 13, 17, 19; such points exist for p = 23 and 31.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`PromislowFracton.Palomar.t2b_signed_haah_64_discriminant_classification`](https://github.com/0thernes/promislow-fracton-amoeba/blob/c829ab811568a13e2283db2175f684e35b17c816/lean/PalomarSolution.lean#L35), [`PromislowFracton.Palomar.t2d_odd_prime_torsion_and_p23_threshold`](https://github.com/0thernes/promislow-fracton-amoeba/blob/c829ab811568a13e2283db2175f684e35b17c816/lean/PalomarSolution.lean#L39), [`PromislowFracton.hasOddOrderRootFp`](https://github.com/0thernes/promislow-fracton-amoeba/blob/c829ab811568a13e2283db2175f684e35b17c816/lean/PromislowFracton/HaahFractons.lean#L729); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound. Code dimensions such as k₂₃(11) = 6 are Rust/Python computations.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement is narrower than the usual informal claim (see Limits); prior work: not yet searched.
- **Limit.** The map from signed codes to conic discriminants is a definition, not derived, and smoothness ⇔ nonzero discriminant is not formalized.
- **Limit.** Points over extension fields F_{p^k} are not covered.
- **Limit.** No code dimension k_p(L) is defined in Lean.
- **Limit.** No literature search was done for these computations, and no source is cited that poses the all-primes question as open.
- **Upstream.** The source repository has commits newer than the archived pin. They are not covered by this audit.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake build && lake env lean VerifyAxioms.lean`

<a id="r59"></a>
### R59. Mod-8 residue locking in one integral octahedral circle packing

- **Statement.** The eight octahedral face reflections are involutions preserving the octahedral Descartes equations. Every sextuple reachable from (−6,10,17,40,24,17) has residues (2,2,1,0,0,1) mod 8; hence b₁, b₂ are never squares and b₃, b₆ are never twice a square. A √−2 norm identity holds for octahedral sextuples.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declaration [`ApollonianSandpileMarkov.Palomar.t1d_octahedral_lorentzian_and_sqrt_neg2_obstruction`](https://github.com/0thernes/apollonian-sandpile-markov/blob/94ece6a8dce9c9a30c6a2ada2eb335cb0501f770/lean/PalomarSolution.lean#L29); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound. The exclusion follows from congruences mod 8; no reciprocity argument is formalized.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement is narrower than the usual informal claim (see Limits); prior work: overlapping earlier or parallel work found (see Limits).
- **Re-audit pending.** Audited at commit `94ece6a`; the archive pin has since moved to `bc6464b`.
- **Limit.** Nothing is shown about squares at b₃–b₆ or twice-squares at b₁, b₂, b₄, b₅.
- **Limit.** The 'Jacobi' conjunct consists of closed numeric evaluations unrelated to the orbit.
- **Limit.** Quadratic reciprocity obstructions for octahedral packings were published earlier by Shi, Shi, Whitehead, Williams-Tracy, and Zhang (arXiv:2510.21702, 2025), which v2.0.0 cites.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake build && lake env lean VerifyAxioms.lean`
- **Source.** [apollonian-sandpile-markov](https://github.com/0thernes/apollonian-sandpile-markov) `v3.0.0` · archive entry `0TH-2026-018` · [paper](papers/0TH-2026-018_apollonian-sandpile-markov/Donahue_2026_apollonian_sandpile_markov.pdf)

<a id="r13"></a>
### R13. Frozen bit strings of the 0110↔1001 dipole-conserving gate

- **Statement.** Swapping 0110 and 1001 preserves charge and dipole moment. The sequence Z with Z(0..4) = 1, 2, 4, 8, 14 and Z(L) = Z(L−1) + Z(L−2) + Z(L−4) for L ≥ 5 equals the number of length-L bit strings avoiding 0110 and 1001 for L = 1, …, 6, and satisfies 2Z(L) < Z(L+4) for L ≥ 1. Over ℤ, x⁴ − x³ − x² − 1 = (x+1)(x³ − 2x² + x − 1).
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`Monodromia.Palomar.t6_dipole_scars_and_plastic_squared_shattering`](https://github.com/0thernes/monodromia/blob/153792bdaee2b01f9a56afbcb60af7d2957d5669/lean/PalomarSolution.lean#L31), [`Monodromia.DipoleScars.dipole_gate_conserves_charge_and_dipole`](https://github.com/0thernes/monodromia/blob/153792bdaee2b01f9a56afbcb60af7d2957d5669/lean/MonodromiaProofs/DipoleScars.lean#L21), [`Monodromia.DipoleScars.exact_frozen_matches_zFrozen_small`](https://github.com/0thernes/monodromia/blob/153792bdaee2b01f9a56afbcb60af7d2957d5669/lean/MonodromiaProofs/DipoleScars.lean#L89), [`Monodromia.DipoleScars.zFrozen_strict_exponential_shattering`](https://github.com/0thernes/monodromia/blob/153792bdaee2b01f9a56afbcb60af7d2957d5669/lean/MonodromiaProofs/DipoleScars.lean#L140); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound. Count identity kernel-checked for L ≤ 6 only; agreement for larger L is computational (L ≤ 14 in the repository, L ≤ 15 in this audit).
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement is narrower than the usual informal claim (see Limits); prior work: overlapping earlier or parallel work found (see Limits).
- **Limit.** The 'Plastic-constant-squared' conjunct is vacuous: ρ³ = ρ + 1 has no integer solution.
- **Limit.** The growth rate ρ² and Θ(ρ^(2L)) are not in Lean.
- **Limit.** 'Skin-effect immunity' of frozen strings follows directly from the definition of frozen.
- **Limit.** The string count is kernel-checked only for L = 1, …, 6. dfa_total_eq_zFrozen proves the recurrence for a four-state transfer count for every L, but that count is never proved equal to the number of bit strings.
- **Limit.** The counting sequence coincides with OEIS A164394 (stated there for the pattern pair 0001, 0100) on L = 0..15; exponentially many frozen states under dipole-conserving dynamics are known (Sala et al. 2020; Khemani et al. 2020).
- **Upstream.** The source repository has commits newer than the archived pin. They are not covered by this audit.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake build && lake env lean VerifyAxioms.lean`
- **Source.** [monodromia](https://github.com/0thernes/monodromia) `v1.0.0` · archive entry `0TH-2026-014` · [paper](papers/0TH-2026-014_monodromia/Donahue_2026_Monodromia.pdf)

<a id="r14"></a>
### R14. Minimum Kochen-Specker set in the 40-ray pool {0, ±1}⁴

- **Statement.** Let B(4,1) be the 40 rays of ℤ⁴ with entries in {0, 1, −1}. Some 18 of them admit no Kochen–Specker colouring (bases are orthogonal quadruples inside the set), and every subset of at most 17 of them admits one.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`KS.Box4H1.minimum_ks_size_vectors`](https://github.com/0thernes/kochen-specker-extremal-lab/blob/4c4dda9113f6d7af037d3ab0d7c4d9ca0191fa84/lean/KS/Box4H1.lean#L335), [`KS.ComparatorTarget.target_box4h1_minimum_18`](https://github.com/0thernes/kochen-specker-extremal-lab/blob/4c4dda9113f6d7af037d3ab0d7c4d9ca0191fa84/lean/Solution.lean#L14); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement matches the stated claim; prior work: known mathematics.
- **Re-audit pending.** Audited at commit `4c4dda9`; the archive pin has since moved to `a433e72`.
- **Limit.** The bound 18 is known for all Kochen–Specker sets in every dimension (Xu, Chen, Gühne, arXiv:2001.07656); this record restricts it to one finite pool.
- **Limit.** That there are exactly 16 minimum sets in the pool is not part of the Lean theorem.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake build KS Solution && lake env lean KS/Audit.lean`

<a id="r15"></a>
### R15. Algebraic closed forms of the Banić–Elezović recombination expressions for γ(n), γ_c(n), γ(3;N), γ(4;N)

- **Statement.** With p₁ = (n²−4n−4)/(32πn), q₁ = a_△·n/π − (n+1)/(8πn), r₁ = −(n+1)/(8πn) and a_△ = 1/32 − 4√3/243: for real n ≠ 0, (n−1)p₁ + 2(n−2)q₁ + (n−2)(n−3)r₁ = −n(n+1)/(32π) − 8√3·n(n−2)/(243π) + (n−1)/(8πn), and n·p₁ + 2n·q₁ + n(n−3)r₁ = −n(n+4)/(32π) − 8√3·n²/(243π). Two analogous identities hold at n = 3 and n = 4.
- **Related source.** [N. Banić, N. Elezović, Discrete total variation under the multinomial, Part 2: variance under the uniform null (2026), arXiv:2610.17046](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17046), Theorem 7.6 and the consequences stated after Conjecture 7.7; for γ_c, Theorem 3.10 of Part 5, arXiv:2611.14735.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`MultinomialTV.ComparatorTarget.target_gamma_closed_form`](https://github.com/0thernes/multinomial-tv-conjecture/blob/0a69443e1b663f0874b547481b7395fdd24732c4/lean/Solution.lean#L143), [`MultinomialTV.ComparatorTarget.target_gamma_cyclic_closed_form`](https://github.com/0thernes/multinomial-tv-conjecture/blob/0a69443e1b663f0874b547481b7395fdd24732c4/lean/Solution.lean#L155), [`MultinomialTV.ComparatorTarget.target_gamma3_closed_form`](https://github.com/0thernes/multinomial-tv-conjecture/blob/0a69443e1b663f0874b547481b7395fdd24732c4/lean/Solution.lean#L166), [`MultinomialTV.ComparatorTarget.target_gamma4_closed_form`](https://github.com/0thernes/multinomial-tv-conjecture/blob/0a69443e1b663f0874b547481b7395fdd24732c4/lean/Solution.lean#L178); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound. Identities between real numbers only. That the left sides are variance coefficients is taken from Banić–Elezović, outside Lean.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement is narrower than the usual informal claim (see Limits); prior work: known mathematics.
- **Re-audit pending.** Audited at commit `0a69443`; the archive pin has since moved to `5b3d510`.
- **Limit.** Lean defines no random variable, variance or expansion in N. That these expressions are the N⁻² coefficients of Var(d)/N rests on Theorem 7.6, Lemmas 7.2, 7.3, 7.5 and Section 8 of arXiv:2610.17046v1 and Theorem 3.10 of arXiv:2611.14735v1, which are unrefereed and not formalized.
- **Limit.** The closed forms are already printed in the source as consequences conditional on Conjecture 7.7; these theorems machine-check that algebra.
- **Limit.** In Lean n is any nonzero real; the source's statements are for integer n ≥ 5, with n = 3 and n = 4 treated separately.
- **Limit.** In the n = 4 identity the term (−1)^N/(2π) appears unchanged on both sides, so the dependence on N is carried, not derived.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake exe cache get && lake build && lake env lean VerifyAxioms.lean`
- **Source.** [multinomial-tv-conjecture](https://github.com/0thernes/multinomial-tv-conjecture) `v2.7.0` · archive entry `0TH-2026-006` · [paper](papers/0TH-2026-006_multinomial-tv-conjecture/Donahue_2026_Conjecture_7_7_Multinomial_TV.pdf)

<a id="r16"></a>
### R16. Inequalities for the Hill function of order four in cleared-denominator form

- **Statement.** For naturals c, c1 < c2, kd > 0, x⁴ written out: c⁴ < c⁴ + kd⁴; c⁴/(c⁴+kd⁴) strictly increases in c; it is at most 1/82 when 3c ≤ kd; for kd = 2h, h > 0, its values at kd and h have ratio 17/2; for c > 0 it lies below c/(c+kd) when c < kd and above when c > kd; and 2·kd⁴ = kd⁴ + kd⁴.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`SynapseBouton.hill4_unit_interval`](https://github.com/0thernes/human-synapse-bouton-lab/blob/06e0dcdd5fc4cb406e9fe21d260ff94b68a414d4/lean/Solution.lean#L114), [`SynapseBouton.hill4_half_max_exact`](https://github.com/0thernes/human-synapse-bouton-lab/blob/06e0dcdd5fc4cb406e9fe21d260ff94b68a414d4/lean/Solution.lean#L119), [`SynapseBouton.hill4_strict_monotone`](https://github.com/0thernes/human-synapse-bouton-lab/blob/06e0dcdd5fc4cb406e9fe21d260ff94b68a414d4/lean/Solution.lean#L123), [`SynapseBouton.hill4_resting_noise_floor`](https://github.com/0thernes/human-synapse-bouton-lab/blob/06e0dcdd5fc4cb406e9fe21d260ff94b68a414d4/lean/Solution.lean#L132), [`SynapseBouton.hill4_ultrasensitive_doubling`](https://github.com/0thernes/human-synapse-bouton-lab/blob/06e0dcdd5fc4cb406e9fe21d260ff94b68a414d4/lean/Solution.lean#L144), [`SynapseBouton.hill4_ultrasensitive_crossover_vs_linear`](https://github.com/0thernes/human-synapse-bouton-lab/blob/06e0dcdd5fc4cb406e9fe21d260ff94b68a414d4/lean/Solution.lean#L169); axioms: propext, Quot.sound (T03: none; T06: propext only).
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement matches the stated claim; prior work: known mathematics.
- **Limit.** Statements are over natural numbers with denominators cleared; no real-valued Hill function or Ca²⁺ model is defined.
- **Limit.** T02 is the identity 2x = x + x and its hypothesis kd > 0 is unused.
- **Limit.** These are textbook properties; a prior public Lean 4 formalisation of Hill monotonicity exists (marpaia/grn-lean, per the literature audit).
- **Upstream.** The source repository has commits newer than the archived pin. They are not covered by this audit.

<a id="r17"></a>
### R17. Integer bounds for a short-term-plasticity step, conductance means, a blocking factor and 2×2 determinants

- **Statement.** Over integers with stated positivity, denominators cleared: one Tsodyks–Markram step u⁺ = u + ⌊u₀(S−u)/S⌋ keeps u ≤ u⁺ ≤ S and releases at most x; eI·G ≤ Σgᵢeᵢ ≤ eE·G, strictly lowered when gI > 0; D/(D+m·e) < 1, decreasing in e; σ²N < σ²NK for K ≥ 2; kc/(kc+kOff) increases in c; rank-one 2×2 tables have det 0; diagonal excess gives |det| > 0.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`SynapseBouton.tsodyks_markram_step_unit_bounds`](https://github.com/0thernes/human-synapse-bouton-lab/blob/06e0dcdd5fc4cb406e9fe21d260ff94b68a414d4/lean/Solution.lean#L192), [`SynapseBouton.ei_steady_state_bounds`](https://github.com/0thernes/human-synapse-bouton-lab/blob/06e0dcdd5fc4cb406e9fe21d260ff94b68a414d4/lean/Solution.lean#L224), [`SynapseBouton.shunting_inhibition_strict_attenuation`](https://github.com/0thernes/human-synapse-bouton-lab/blob/06e0dcdd5fc4cb406e9fe21d260ff94b68a414d4/lean/Solution.lean#L249), [`SynapseBouton.pure_shunting_divisive_identity`](https://github.com/0thernes/human-synapse-bouton-lab/blob/06e0dcdd5fc4cb406e9fe21d260ff94b68a414d4/lean/Solution.lean#L266), [`SynapseBouton.nmda_mg_unblock_unit_interval_and_monotone`](https://github.com/0thernes/human-synapse-bouton-lab/blob/06e0dcdd5fc4cb406e9fe21d260ff94b68a414d4/lean/Solution.lean#L281), [`SynapseBouton.resi_variance_strict_improvement`](https://github.com/0thernes/human-synapse-bouton-lab/blob/06e0dcdd5fc4cb406e9fe21d260ff94b68a414d4/lean/Solution.lean#L404), [`SynapseBouton.resi_duty_cycle_monotone_in_imager`](https://github.com/0thernes/human-synapse-bouton-lab/blob/06e0dcdd5fc4cb406e9fe21d260ff94b68a414d4/lean/Solution.lean#L413), [`SynapseBouton.factorized_bipartition_zero_integration`](https://github.com/0thernes/human-synapse-bouton-lab/blob/06e0dcdd5fc4cb406e9fe21d260ff94b68a414d4/lean/Solution.lean#L427), [`SynapseBouton.correlated_nanocolumn_positive_integration`](https://github.com/0thernes/human-synapse-bouton-lab/blob/06e0dcdd5fc4cb406e9fe21d260ff94b68a414d4/lean/Solution.lean#L437), [`SynapseBouton.diagonal_excess_implies_positive_coupling`](https://github.com/0thernes/human-synapse-bouton-lab/blob/06e0dcdd5fc4cb406e9fe21d260ff94b68a414d4/lean/Solution.lean#L446); axioms: propext, Quot.sound (T21: none; T23: propext only).
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement is narrower than the usual informal claim (see Limits); prior work: known mathematics.
- **Limit.** T11 is monotonicity in an abstract natural e; the voltage dependence of NMDA Mg²⁺ unblock (exp(−0.062 V)) is not formalised.
- **Limit.** T10 states two numerator identities; the divisive ratio (gL+gE)/(gL+gE+gI) is not itself a Lean statement.

<a id="r18"></a>
### R18. Clean product of 2^k inputs by a balanced four-step telescoping recursion

- **Statement.** For every k, sign ±1 and duplicate-free register list target :: pool with |pool| ≥ k+1 when k ≥ 1, a program of (7·4^k − 4)/3 instructions of the forms R_i ± x_l and R_i ± R_a·R_b (i, a, b pairwise distinct) adds ± x_off · … · x_(off+2^k−1) to the target register and leaves every other register unchanged, over every commutative ring and from every initial state.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`Catalytic.Solution.t4_balanced_multilinear_product`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Solution.lean#L93), [`Catalytic.Challenge.T4_BalancedMultilinearProduct`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Challenge.lean#L229), [`Catalytic.balancedProdProg`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Catalytic/BalancedProduct.lean#L75), [`Catalytic.balancedProdProg_clean`](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers/blob/4bfeabb05a960450114f975f477259ccf189cdf1/lean/Catalytic/BalancedProduct.lean#L258); axioms: propext, Quot.sound. Variant of the classical four-term clean-product recursion; the exact instruction count in this instruction set was not located in the literature.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement is narrower than the usual informal claim (see Limits); prior work: overlapping earlier or parallel work found (see Limits).
- **Limit.** The publicly quoted register count log₂ d + 2 is not a conjunct of the Lean statement at the audited commit; it is read off the program definition (the conjunct was added upstream after this commit).
- **Limit.** Overlap with earlier work: Ben-Or and Cleve (SIAM J. Comput. 1992) give polynomial-length three-register programs for all polynomial-size formulas in a neighbouring instruction set and report earlier logarithmic-register methods; the paper does not position this result against them.
- **Limit.** No lower bound is proved; the words 'optimal' and 'sharp' used for this result in the paper are unsupported.
- **Limit.** Number of inputs is a power of two.
- **Upstream.** The source repository has commits newer than the archived pin. They are not covered by this audit.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake build && lake env lean VerifyAxioms.lean`
- **Source.** [catalytic-register-frontiers](https://github.com/0thernes/catalytic-register-frontiers) `v1.0.1` · archive entry `0TH-2026-010` · [paper](papers/0TH-2026-010_catalytic-register-frontiers/Donahue_2026_catalytic_register_frontiers.pdf)

<a id="r19"></a>
### R19. ML-KEM parameter arithmetic and a Compress_1 decoding margin

- **Statement.** 3329 is prime and 17 has multiplicative order 256 modulo 3329; signed Montgomery reduction returns r with r·2^16 ≡ a (mod 3329) and |r| < 3329 for |a| < 2^15·3329; for b ∈ {0,1} and |e| ≤ 831 the repository's Lean function mlkemCompress1 maps b·1665 + e to b.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`SecMeshPQSI.Solution.t5a_fips203_ntt_prime_and_root`](https://github.com/0thernes/secmesh-pqsi/blob/6c7f6421465c0018f2cfa385ede6f1041a62e543/lean/Solution.lean#L83), [`SecMeshPQSI.Solution.t5b_montgomery_reduction_exact`](https://github.com/0thernes/secmesh-pqsi/blob/6c7f6421465c0018f2cfa385ede6f1041a62e543/lean/Solution.lean#L86), [`SecMeshPQSI.Solution.t5c_mlkem_compress1_margin`](https://github.com/0thernes/secmesh-pqsi/blob/6c7f6421465c0018f2cfa385ede6f1041a62e543/lean/Solution.lean#L89); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound. MlkemCompress1 uses offset 1665 and differs from FIPS 203 Compress_1 only at x = 832, which this margin statement never reaches.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement is narrower than the usual informal claim (see Limits); prior work: known mathematics.
- **Limit.** The Lean Compress_1 model is not FIPS 203: it returns 1 at x = 832 where FIPS 203 returns 0.
- **Limit.** The companion claim that both b = 0 and b = 1 fail at e = +832 (adv_compress1_fails_at_plus_832, the CI negative test, the paper) is false for FIPS 203; only b = 1 fails.
- **Limit.** No statement about ML-KEM security or full decryption correctness is formalized.
- **Limit.** The margin condition is the standard Kyber/ML-KEM correctness condition and has earlier machine-checked treatments.
- **Upstream.** The source repository has commits newer than the archived pin (latest release `v3.0.1`). They are not covered by this audit.
- **Check it.** `cd lean && lake build && lake env lean VerifyAxioms.lean`

<a id="r20"></a>
### R20. Combinatorial cores of one-time pad, XOR combining, Toeplitz hashing, Shamir sharing and Reed–Solomon decoding

- **Statement.** Over Boolean vectors: the one-time-pad key and each XOR-combiner factor are unique given the rest; for x ≠ y, some m-bit Toeplitz seed window, given the other seed bits, is fixed uniquely by each target hash difference. Over any SimpleField: a nonzero polynomial with ≤ d+1 coefficients has ≤ d distinct roots; t points determine f(0) for length ≤ t; t−1 shares fit every secret; f(0) is uniquely decodable when n ≥ t + 2b.
- **Evidence.** Lean kernel; declarations [`SecMeshPQSI.Solution.t2a_shannon_otp_perfect_secrecy`](https://github.com/0thernes/secmesh-pqsi/blob/6c7f6421465c0018f2cfa385ede6f1041a62e543/lean/Solution.lean#L41), [`SecMeshPQSI.Solution.t2b_hybrid_dual_survivability`](https://github.com/0thernes/secmesh-pqsi/blob/6c7f6421465c0018f2cfa385ede6f1041a62e543/lean/Solution.lean#L44), [`SecMeshPQSI.Solution.t2c1_toeplitz_exact_2_universal`](https://github.com/0thernes/secmesh-pqsi/blob/6c7f6421465c0018f2cfa385ede6f1041a62e543/lean/Solution.lean#L47), [`SecMeshPQSI.Solution.t2d_carter_wegman_poly_bound`](https://github.com/0thernes/secmesh-pqsi/blob/6c7f6421465c0018f2cfa385ede6f1041a62e543/lean/Solution.lean#L53), [`SecMeshPQSI.Solution.t3a_shamir_threshold_and_secrecy`](https://github.com/0thernes/secmesh-pqsi/blob/6c7f6421465c0018f2cfa385ede6f1041a62e543/lean/Solution.lean#L56), [`SecMeshPQSI.Solution.t3b_byzantine_singleton_tightness`](https://github.com/0thernes/secmesh-pqsi/blob/6c7f6421465c0018f2cfa385ede6f1041a62e543/lean/Solution.lean#L59); axioms: propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound.
- **Audit (2026-11-26).** Rebuilt from a clean clone at the audited commit: pass; the formal statement is narrower than the usual informal claim (see Limits); prior work: known mathematics.
- **Limit.** Probabilistic secrecy, universal-hash collision probability and the leftover hash lemma are not formalized; only bijection and counting cores are.
- **Limit.** The 'tightness' part of t3b is only the arithmetic d = 2b; no counterexample is built.
- **Limit.** All are textbook results (Shannon 1949, Carter–Wegman, Shamir 1979, Reed–Solomon decoding).
